# Environment variables

The code reads environment names through `getenv`, `IsEnvironmentSet`, `kibSetting`, `MaskFromEnvironment`, `EnvInt`, `GetEnvironmentVariableA`, `GetEnvironmentVariableW` and `_wgetenv` with a wide literal, `configured`, `PipelineSwitch`, `environmentCount`, `EnvironmentNumber`, `MebibytesFromEnvironment` and `addWideString` (the wide names the Windows entry stub reads). This table lists every `APS5_*` and `ANYPS5_*` name it reads, its kind, the value used when it is unset and what it does, one table per library. Regenerate the names with `python3 tools/env_reference.py --table`; the Conventions check fails when a name the code reads has no row here, or a row's name is no longer read.

### core/libs

| Name | Kind | Source | Default | Description |
|------|------|--------|---------|-------------|
| `ANYPS5_GUEST_ENV_TEST_4C27` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestEnvironment.cpp` |  | test variable that the guest environment test sets, copies and removes; not a product setting |
| `ANYPS5_JOB_MANAGER_CASE` | string | `core/libs/tests/JobManagerReference.cpp` |  | selects the CPU fixture run by `tools/run_job_manager_reference.py` |
| `ANYPS5_JOB_MANAGER_HOST` | presence | `core/libs/tests/JobManagerReference.cpp` |  | runs the reference adapter against the host fallback instead of the loaded guest module |
| `ANYPS5_LOADEXEC_PARENT` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestLoadExecHost.cpp` |  | variable the Windows entry stub reads; its wide name is built in `core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/windows/WindowsEntryStubBuilder.cpp` |
| `ANYPS5_KERNEL_SYS_EXEC_MODE` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestExecHost.cpp` |  | selects `_execve` (`ve`) or `_execvpe` (`vpe`) in the exec replacement test fixture; unset tests libkernel `execve` |
| `ANYPS5_LOADEXEC_TEST_ROOT` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestLoadExecHost.cpp` |  | directory the load-exec host test reads its guest executable from |
| `ANYPS5_POSIX_SPAWN_ROLE` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestPosixSpawn.cpp` |  | role of the test's own copy spawned as a `posix_spawn` child, which exits or sleeps on it and otherwise fails |
| `ANYPS5_TEST_QUOTED_ARGV0` | presence | `core/libs/tests/GuestLoadExecHost.cpp` |  | makes the load-exec test's first argument one with quotes, a backslash and non-ASCII text |
| `APS5_EBOOT` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceSystemService/Export.cpp` |  | host path of the executable launched for /app0/eboot.bin; unset or empty means the running executable |
| `APS5_LOAD_EXEC_STANDARD_ERROR` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestSystemServiceLoadExecStandardError.cpp` |  | marker that the standard-error load-exec test sets in its child process to choose a mode, `list` or `none`; not a product setting |
| `APS5_SKIP_TIMING_TESTS` | string | `core/libs/tests/PreciseWait.cpp` |  | treats the timing tests' runner as preempted, so they skip without probing the clock; an empty value or one starting with 0 leaves the probe on |
| `APS5_USBD_DEVICE` | string | `core/libs/tests/GuestUsbd.cpp` |  | selects the USB device a test drives, as vendor:product in hex |

### core/relinker

| Name | Kind | Source | Default | Description |
|------|------|--------|---------|-------------|
| `ANYPS5_AUTORUN_NO_PAUSE` | string | `core/relinker/cli/src/Autorun.cpp` | | exactly `1` skips the exit prompt and stdin read after autorun; unset, empty and other values keep the prompt |
| `ANYPS5_GUEST_MODULE_ARGS` | string | `core/relinker/elfpatcher/tests/GuestModuleArgsHost.cpp` |  | path of the file the module-argument fixture appends its events to |
| `APS5_RELINK_REDZONE_SKIPS` | presence | `core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/RedZoneFramer.cpp` |  | with `--frame-red-zone`, prints a `red-zone skip` line on stderr for each red-zone leaf the in-place framing refuses, with its address range and reason (and the offset and bytes of the instruction the reason names), and with APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_RED_ZONE a `red-zone not relocated` line with the reason for each such leaf whose relocation is refused too |
| `APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_RANGE` | string | `core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/RedZoneFramer.cpp` |  | `<first>[:<end>]`, decimal or `0x` hex: with APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_RED_ZONE, relocates only the refused red-zone leaves whose index among the refused leaves, counted from 0, is at least `<first>` and below `<end>` (no upper bound without `:<end>`); the others are skipped as without relocation |
| `APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_RED_ZONE` | presence | `core/relinker/relinker/src/pipeline/Conversion.cpp` |  | with `--frame-red-zone`, relocates a red-zone leaf the in-place framing refuses whole into a framed stub instead of skipping it, unless the relocation refuses it too; the conversion log counts the relocated leaves and the refusals by reason |
| `APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_TRACE` | integer | `core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/RedZoneFramer.cpp` |  | start address of a red-zone leaf (`0x` for hex), as its `red-zone skip` line prints it, whose relocation under APS5_RELINK_RELOCATE_RED_ZONE prints one `reloc` line per instruction on stderr with the instruction's bytes and the stub bytes it became |
| `APS5_RELINK_STUB_MAP` | presence | `core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/windows/WindowsTrampolineBuilder.cpp` |  | while the `.amdstub` section of a Windows image is built, prints a line on stderr per trampoline site with its stub's RVA range, the site's RVA and length, its relocation count and the offset of its return branch |

### core/shader

`APS5_LOOP_GUARD=<exits>` limits evaluated loop exits per shader invocation (`core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvEmitter.cpp`); `0` or an unset variable disables that limit. Its presence separately enables the AGC fault buffer, as listed under `libSceAgcDriver`.

| Name | Kind | Source | Default | Description |
|------|------|--------|---------|-------------|
| `ANYPS5_NO_SHADER_CACHE` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/ShaderDiskCache.cpp` |  | disables the on-disk shader cache when set to a non-empty value other than 0 |
| `ANYPS5_SHADER_CACHE_DIR` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/ShaderDiskCache.cpp` |  | directory for the on-disk shader cache, overriding the one beside the executable |
| `APS5_BDA_BYTE_READS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvBdaRead.cpp` |  | forces one address lookup and byte load per byte for BDA reads instead of a wide dword load |
| `APS5_BDA_DWORD_STORES` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/FlatInstructions.cpp` |  | reverts BDA writes to one address store per dword instead of a wide multi-dword store |
| `APS5_COHERENT_ATOMIC_LOADS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvRuntimeBuffer.cpp` |  | emits `glc` buffer loads as device-scope relaxed atomic loads instead of volatile loads, for compute programs that wait for another workgroup's flag ([shader debt](debt/shader.md)) |
| `APS5_DUMP_IR` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | hex code address (or all) of the programs whose IR is printed after each front-end pass |
| `APS5_EXACT_RCP` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterReciprocal.cpp` |  | emits `v_rcp_f32` and `v_rcp_iflag_f32` as the RDNA hardware reciprocal (bit-exact for normal inputs and results) instead of the driver's division; adds about 13k SPIR-V words to each shader that uses them |
| `APS5_FAST_F32` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, compiles f32 arithmetic with the host's float rules instead of RDNA's: no emulated denormal flush and no RDNA NaN payloads, which differ only for denormals and NaN results; the shader disk cache keeps the two modes apart |
| `APS5_GATHER_CONST` | integer | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp` |  | integer every non-depth gather returns in all four texels instead of the sampled value |
| `APS5_HOST_F32_FMA_NANS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, the startup f32 NaN probe also compares the three-source multiply-add forms (`v_fma_f32`, `v_fmac_f32`, `v_fmamk_f32`, `v_fmaak_f32`, with constant factors and addends); when none differs and the two-source forms keep the device's NaN, a multiply-add with IEEE_MODE clear keeps the device's result behind at most two selects (a variable factor's NaN, and the addend where a denormal factor meets an infinity) instead of the full NaN selection; a multiply-add of three constants keeps the full selection. Exact where the probe proves the device: the probe runs as a compute program and, as with the two-source forms, its verdict applies to every stage. No effect with `APS5_NO_HOST_F32_NANS`, which skips the probe. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. Log line `[float-controls] f32 multiply-add NaN results` |
| `APS5_IMAGE_TABLE_KEY_SCAN` | integer | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp` | 256 | number of image-table entries the key scan examines |
| `APS5_IMAGE_TABLE_STRICT` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp` |  | sets image-table materialization to its strict form; 0 leaves it off |
| `APS5_NATIVE_F32_MINMAX` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, a startup probe compares GLSL `NMin` and `NMax` with the selected f32 min, max, min3, max3 and med3 forms over NaN payloads, signed zeros, infinities and denormals in six float modes (IEEE_MODE set and clear); when no result differs, those instructions are emitted with `NMin` and `NMax` (plus the NaN selects the guest's NaN rules need) instead of bit sequences. An instruction whose first two operands are both constants keeps the bit sequence, except a med3 that clamps a variable between two constants. Exact where the probe proves the device: the probe runs as a compute program and its verdict applies to every stage. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. Log line `[float-controls] f32 min, max and med3` |
| `APS5_NO_ATOMIC_BARRIER` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvFormatConvert.cpp` |  | drops the device-scope acquire/release memory barrier emitted after atomics |
| `APS5_NO_ATOMIC_ZERO_SKIP` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp` |  | restores plain buffer atomics instead of skipping identity-operand atomics |
| `APS5_NO_BUFFER_SUBSTITUTES` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | specializes a null buffer V# with its own words instead of the fields its slot had the first time a dispatch of the same program bound it |
| `APS5_NO_CODE_HASH_KEY` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/CacheKey.hpp` |  | appends every code word to the cache key instead of hashing the code |
| `APS5_NO_FAILURE_MEMO` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | rebuilds a program whose compilation failed instead of reusing the memoized failure |
| `APS5_NO_FAULT_CHECK_DEDUP` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvRuntimeBuffer.cpp` |  | emits every invalid-descriptor fault check of a GPU-descriptor buffer access instead of skipping one that an identical check on the same descriptor under the same execution guard already covers in the same IR block or a dominating block; read once per program |
| `APS5_NO_IMAGE_TABLES` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceTracker.cpp` |  | disables image tables, leaving table-loaded descriptors unplanned |
| `APS5_NO_MASKED_RESULT_MEMO` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | materializes every result memo miss instead of patching the buffer descriptors, user data and flattened SRT of a cached result whose snapshot differs only in those words |
| `APS5_NO_NAN_PAYLOAD_ELIMINATION` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | keeps every f32 NaN-result select, including those whose NaN payload no store, export, branch or bit operation can observe |
| `APS5_NO_NATIVE_F16_NANS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | skips the startup f16 conversion probe and keeps the NaN repairs of every native f16 to f32 and f32 to f16 conversion |
| `APS5_NO_PERF_DISK_PLANS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | disables the disk cache of planning results |
| `APS5_NO_PERF_DOMINATOR_TREE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/Structurizer.cpp` |  | computes the structurizer's dominator and post-dominator sets with the set fixpoint instead of a dominator tree, walks every selection construct instead of testing its members by dominance, and tests every block against each construct when checking the structured constructs instead of walking the construct |
| `APS5_NO_PERF_DOM_INTERSECTION` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/Structurizer.cpp` |  | computes dominator intersections into a fresh vector instead of intersecting in place |
| `APS5_NO_PERF_ITERATION_SELECTION` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/Structurizer.cpp` |  | recomputes the post-dominator sets when finding a loop's iteration-selection merge instead of reusing them |
| `APS5_NO_RESULT_MEMO` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | materializes a fresh result for every recompile instead of reusing the result memo |
| `APS5_NO_SCOPED_VECTOR_WINDOW` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp` |  | keeps a program's vector registers in its relative-addressing window in every block instead of only in the blocks between relative accesses |
| `APS5_NO_SRT_TAPE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtTape.cpp` |  | runs the runtime-source walk of a plan in the IR evaluator instead of the compiled tape |
| `APS5_NO_TABLE_RESOLUTION_REUSE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp` |  | stops a result memo miss from reusing an unchanged image table's resolution, so the table is resolved again |
| `APS5_NO_TABLE_SNAPSHOT_REUSE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp` |  | turns off the image table snapshot reuse, so every capture reads and compares each table column |
| `APS5_NO_WINDOW_NARROWING` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvModuleSetup.cpp` |  | indexes every slot of the shader's vector window instead of only the slots the program uses |
| `APS5_PROBE` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | pc:vgpr[:shift] probe that copies that VGPR into v255 after the instruction so image stores write it as a per-lane trace |
| `APS5_RAYTRACING` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | selects the image_bvh_intersect_ray behaviour: strict throws and miss writes 0xffffffff |
| `APS5_READONLY_GUEST_BUFFERS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, declares the guest buffer descriptor array `NonWritable` in shaders that write no guest buffer, unless the array itself carries `Coherent` (a shader with coherent buffer accesses and no separate coherent alias of the array). Results are unchanged; off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart |
| `APS5_RESOURCE_PLAN_MB` | integer | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` | 0 | MiB the resource plans of drawn shaders may hold before the least recently used plans that nothing else holds are dropped and reloaded from the shader disk cache at their next use (0 keeps every plan) |
| `APS5_RESULT_MEMO_MB` | integer | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` | 256 | MiB the result memos of all shader sources may hold before the least recently used results are dropped (0 keeps them until their set evicts them) |
| `APS5_SAVEEXEC_WRITE_FIRST` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ControlFlowInstructions.cpp` |  | writes the saved exec to the destination before reading the source in s_*_saveexec |
| `APS5_SINGLE_LANE` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | hex code addresses (comma separated, or all) of wave64 programs kept at one guest lane per invocation |
| `APS5_SRT_DEBUG` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/RequestMemoryView.cpp` |  | prints extra SRT descriptor-walk debug output, such as guest reads outside the request memory |
| `APS5_STRICT_DESCRIPTOR_WEBS` | string | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceTracker.cpp` |  | makes a T# or S# read in a descriptor web throw when a path's descriptor dwords depend on the program counter, EXEC, a lane mask, the lane index or an image read or sample result (not a gather or an image atomic), instead of selecting the first descriptor and recording an `InvalidDescriptor` fault when a lane takes that path; 0 leaves it off |
| `APS5_TRACE_BDA` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceTracker.cpp` |  | prints the first few accesses that make a program take the address-based path |
| `APS5_TRACE_BUFFER_WRITTEN` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/DescriptorBindingBuilder.cpp` |  | prints how many guest buffer elements a program may store to and how many it only loads |
| `APS5_TRACE_IMAGE_TABLE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp` |  | traces image-table resolution |
| `APS5_VERIFY_MASKED_RESULT_MEMO` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp` |  | also materializes every patched result memo hit, prints the first twenty whose patched result differs and counts them in the result memo report of `APS5_PROFILE_DRAW` |
| `APS5_VERIFY_SRT_COMPACT` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp` |  | walks every SRT descriptor a second time through the IR evaluator and throws when the two walks differ |
| `APS5_VERIFY_SRT_TAPE` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtTape.cpp` |  | walks a plan's runtime sources a second time through the evaluator, adopts its answers where the two differ and prints the first twenty differences |
| `APS5_WAIT_PENDING_BRANCHES` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/ShaderMemory.cpp` |  | a resource capture that evaluates a scalar branch condition over a word with a pending GPU write waits for the write to prune the branch, instead of leaving the branch unpruned (both successors' resources captured) |
| `APS5_WAVE_LDS_SUBGROUP` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvEmitter.cpp` |  | restores subgroup-scope LDS barriers for single-wave workgroups instead of workgroup scope |
| `APS5_XP_NO_SHADER_FAULTS` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/DispatchInstructions.cpp` |  | experiment, a measurement aid and not correct behaviour: shaders are emitted without their fault-buffer reports (invalid descriptors, overflows, unaligned accesses, loop limits, image-table poison, unlinked calls, spilled lanes) and without the fault-buffer store of a poisoned guarded SRT slot, to measure what shader-side fault reporting costs the GPU. A guarded invocation still stops, but the driver never learns of a fault. Read once at start; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. |

| Name | Kind | Source | Default | Description |
|------|------|--------|---------|-------------|
| `APS5_TRACE_LABEL` | integer | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Cb/src/Sync.cpp`, `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Synchronization/LabelHistory.cpp` | 0 | hex guest address; logs a matching RELEASE_MEM when recorded, and nearby RELEASE_MEM, WRITE_DATA, COPY_DATA, DMA_DATA and WAIT_REG_MEM packets when executed |

### libSceAgcDriver

| Name | Kind | Source | Default | Description |
|------|------|--------|---------|-------------|
| `ANYPS5_FPS_LIMIT` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/include/FrameLimit.hpp` | 0 | the most frames per second the window presents, from 0.5 to 1000000 with at most three decimals; 0, empty or unset is no limit, and any other value is refused |
| `ANYPS5_FSR_SHARPNESS` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` | 0.2 | RCAS sharpness in stops under `ANYPS5_UPSCALE=fsr`, from 0.0 (sharpest) to 2.0 (weakest); a value outside that range or not a number is refused |
| `ANYPS5_GPU` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | restricts physical device selection to device names containing this case-insensitive substring |
| `ANYPS5_HOST_REPORT` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` | 0 | prints the host report to stderr once when the presenting device is chosen: 1 prints it; 0, empty or unset prints nothing, and any other value stops the start with an error naming the variable |
| `ANYPS5_OVERLAY` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/PerformanceOverlay.cpp` | 0 | the performance overlay's page at start: 1 the overlay, 2 its detailed page; 0 or unset hides it, and any other value is refused |
| `ANYPS5_OVERLAY_SCALE` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/PerformanceOverlay.cpp` | automatic | a whole number from 1 to 8 sets the overlay's text scale. The scale is taken as requested when the panel fits the window, and shrinks until it does otherwise. Any other value, unset or empty keeps the automatic scale from the window height |
| `ANYPS5_OVERLAY_POS` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/PerformanceOverlay.cpp` | tl | the overlay's corner: tl, tr, bl or br |
| `ANYPS5_PAD_STATUS` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/PadStatusOverlay.cpp` | 0 | 1 shows the controller status panel for 4 seconds when a controller connects, disconnects or enters a lower battery class; 0 or unset never shows it on its own (the `ShowPadStatus` key still does), and any other value is refused |
| `ANYPS5_PRESENT_MODE` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` | fifo | selects the swapchain present mode: fifo, fifo_latest_ready, mailbox or immediate |
| `ANYPS5_REQUIRE_DESCRIPTOR_BINDING_FLAGS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/ComputePipelineReplay.cpp` |  | fails the replay test instead of accepting a device that refuses descriptor binding flags |
| `ANYPS5_REQUIRE_ROBUST2` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/LdsMixedWidthAtomics.cpp` |  | fails the LDS lock-budget test when the device does not enable robustBufferAccess2, instead of only printing it |
| `ANYPS5_REQUIRE_VULKAN` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/BdaDevice.cpp` |  | fails a test instead of skipping when no usable Vulkan device is present |
| `ANYPS5_SCREENSHOTS` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Screenshot.cpp` |  | directory the screenshot key saves its PNG files to, defaulting to anyps5-screenshots beside the executable |
| `ANYPS5_TEST_HOST_IMPORT_ALIGNMENT` | integer | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/Recorder.cpp` |  | stands in for the device's minimum host import alignment in every recorder test when the device has `VK_EXT_external_memory_host`: `0` makes host imports unavailable to them, and a nonzero value must be a multiple of the device's own |
| `ANYPS5_UPSCALE` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | fsr scales the presentation with FSR 1 (EASU and RCAS) when the window is larger than the display buffer; unset or empty keeps the bilinear scaler, and any other value is refused |
| `APS5_AB` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | `<seconds>:<switch>[+<switch>...]`: alternates phase A (switches as the environment sets them) and phase B (the listed runtime switches toggled) at flip packets, each phase at least `<seconds>` long; today's packet loop and the lean segment loop read one A/B clock that starts at the first flip, and a flip whose time comes before the previous flip's fails; prints `[ab]` lines; a name that is not a runtime switch fails the first flip |
| `APS5_AB_FIRST_B` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | with APS5_AB, the first phase is B instead of A, so a profile can cover the B arm alone when the phase outlasts the run |
| `APS5_AB_SETTLE` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` | 2 | seconds into a phase after which APS5_AB prints the phase's `settled` line; shorter than the phase |
| `APS5_AB_START` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` | 0 | flip packet count (from 1) at which APS5_AB starts, or `<n>s` for seconds after the first flip |
| `APS5_ADDRESS_COPY_MAX_MIB` | integer | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp` | 64 | upper bound in MiB on the guest memory one address-based build copies; exceeding it aborts |
| `APS5_ALL_BUFFERS_WRITTEN` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp` |  | notes every shader buffer element as written, as before bufferWritten existed |
| `APS5_ARM_FLIP_RESET` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | with resident arming on, a change of APS5_ARM_SPANS or APS5_ARM_WIDE (an APS5_AB flip; the first write walk after start-up is not one) disarms every armed resident run at the next write walk, and when APS5_ARM_SPANS is off after the change it also marks the runs with views written under it as written, as the default rule would have, so each phase arms under its own rule; the disarms count as resumes on the `[arming]` line |
| `APS5_APPEND_COMMITS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | an express commit whose pass is open under the record's pass key takes a short front: its targets are held by their cache state, pass note and upload version, its depth view by the pass's depth memo, its in-place inputs by the host import and mapping generations of the finisher's preparation, and its inputs' and buffers' ranges by the count table of zones that hold a pending GPU write (with the template's own writes and the unit shadows asked as before); the prepared inputs stay with the hit, their ranges are noted as pending reads, and the rest of the express commit (selection, staging, aliases, pass access, splice, notes, keeps) runs unchanged. Any condition that fails takes the full express commit. `APS5_VERIFY_APPEND_COMMITS=N` sends every N-th accepted commit through the full express commit and counts those it refused, reproved, rechecked or refreshed (`[append-commits-verify]`); `[append-commits]` counts appended and full commits; APS5_AB can toggle it |

| `APS5_CLEAN_CURRENCY` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | a template whose last full proof found the guest memory of every texture it binds (table elements and DCC keys included) inside remembered clean (armed, unwritten) ranges, none of them stamped by a collect or driver store after the tracker generation read before that proof, with no storage images, depth copies or own GPU writes, keeps its proof without revalidating while no clean range was dropped and the texture release, pending storage, host import, guest allocation, unwatch and depth-surface serials hold (buffers read through addresses are read live by the GPU and are not part of the proof); on a hit, imported buffer regions still get their depth-plane flush and pending-write completion, staging decisions are still checked and staging copies still recorded; a dropped clean range costs one clean-range query and one check that no collect or driver store stamped the ranges' 64 KiB tracker blocks after the tracker generation read before the recorded proof, instead of the full proof, so a range that was written, dropped and armed again by another collect goes to the full proof; pending copied writes over its ranges, moved registered views or a failed query send it to the full proof; a proof of the template outside this path that fails drops its clean record; the committer half of APS5_STAGE_PLANS proves planned draws this way without the switch unless APS5_NO_PLAN_CLEAN_PROOFS is set; prints `[clean-currency]` lines; APS5_AB can toggle it |

| `APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | while APS5_DIRECT_BUFFERS is set, the draw finisher that prepared a direct hit's buffers also copies their block into upload memory of its own (a 1 MiB chunk per finisher thread, held only by the hits and the batches that use it, so it is freed when the last batch that binds a slice of it completed and every unbound hit is gone) and builds the draw's bindings object around that slice; the committer, once the prepared buffers pass its tests (the same alignment, depth-guard and unmoved-range tests as without the switch), binds that object instead of allocating one, taking an upload slice and copying the block, and goes on as before (sets written, pushes, keeps); a hit whose buffers are refused, whose slice was made for another device, or that is not committed through its prepared buffers drops its slice unbound; the fifth `[direct-commits]` line of APS5_PIPELINE_STATS counts placed, bound and returned slices, refusals, bytes not copied, objects not allocated and the nanoseconds of a sample of binding steps by route; APS5_AB can toggle it |
| `APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS_DYNAMIC` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | with APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS, a record commit that would write a descriptor set of its template per draw binds instead a pipeline whose layout gives that set's per-draw storage buffer bindings the dynamic storage buffer type: for an unpushed draw both sets (one pipeline per template and record beside the template's own; a record that holds a push pipeline takes it too when a commit moves nothing in the pushed set), for a pushed draw with a written second set the second set beside the unchanged push set (one more pipeline per template and record, made after the record's first commit that wrote that set; with APS5_DIRECT_REPEAT a pushed draw keeps its push pipeline unless the record's last repeat block was refused before the keep test, for a reason the pipeline does not change); the sets are written once per distinct tuple of buffer objects and ranges (a buffer read in place also by its offset; at most 8 tuples kept per template and set) and each draw passes its slice offsets at bind time; the finisher then places all draws' blocks in a ring of at most 8 persistent 16 MiB upload chunks shared by the finisher threads, a chunk being reused only when nothing but the ring holds it (no batch that binds a slice of it is unfinished and no unbound hit holds one), so a tuple matches again when the ring comes round to its chunk; when every ring chunk is busy a chunk outside the ring is used and freed as before; templates with more dynamic buffers than the device's `maxDescriptorSetStorageBuffersDynamic` in the layout used, with heap stages, update-after-bind or variable-count buffer bindings, address tables, fault slots, replaced textures, or per-draw buffers in a binding of another type keep today's written sets; an offset that is not aligned to `minStorageBufferOffsetAlignment` or does not fit 32 bits sends that draw to today's route; the fifth `[direct-commits]` line counts unpushed and pushed draws bound this way, tuple hits and misses (sets written), draws refused, templates over the limit, the nanoseconds of a sample of written-set binding steps by kind (pushed or unpushed, today or dynamic) and the upload ring (chunks, reuses, additions, overflow chunks, most chunks busy at a rotation) and every draw with a written set that left this route, unpushed and pushed apart, by the first reason found (no prepared buffers, the template, the pipeline, the record's first commit, the repeat route, a rebound template); | `APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS_STAMPS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | with APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS, the committer's test of a prepared draw's unmoved ranges (the template's own writes and the recorder's pending writes over each selected allocation) is answered from the template's stamp of the last passing test of the same allocation while the recorder (its id), the recorder's write note count and the template's write list are unchanged since (pending-write ranges are only added with a new note and only removed when a batch retires); and, with APS5_GUARD_RANGE_MEMO, the guard walk over the draw's moved buffers (resident depth surfaces and multisampled targets) is reduced to the multisample test of the moved guest ranges in allocation order when the template's guard memos hold and the prepared buffers passed the committer's depth-guard test (which covered each moved range's whole view); the fifth `[direct-commits]` line counts the tests, the ones answered by stamp and the guard walks answered this way; APS5_AB can toggle it |
| `APS5_COMMIT_FRAGMENTS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | with APS5_COMMIT_BINDINGS, the draw finisher that placed a direct hit's bindings also records the draw's commands into a fragment of its own (the pipeline and its dynamic state, the bindings with any descriptor set the draw writes taken fresh from the descriptor cache, the push constants, the vertex and index buffers and the draw) against the record's published emit block; the committer runs the ordered part of the commit as before (proof, inputs, targets, the prepared selection's tests, keeps), checks that the fragment was made against the objects it chose, begins the pass where one is due and splices the fragment into the batch stream (or replays it into the batch's command buffer without APS5_DEFERRED_ENCODE), or drops it and records today's way |

| `APS5_DIRECT_CLEAN_PROOFS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | while APS5_DIRECT_DRAWS is set, the committer keeps a clean-currency proof of a direct draw's template (APS5_CLEAN_CURRENCY's rule, as the committer half of APS5_STAGE_PLANS does for planned draws) instead of proving it in full on every commit; the `[direct-commits]` line of APS5_PIPELINE_STATS counts the proofs skipped, not skipped and run in full; APS5_VERIFY_CLEAN_CURRENCY checks the kept proofs |

| `APS5_DIRECT_BUFFERS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | while APS5_DIRECT_DRAWS is set, a direct draw's buffers are prepared on the finisher: the worker reads the switch with APS5_DIRECT_DRAWS from the lean segment's switch copy and marks the job; the committer, after a recorded commit of a direct hit, publishes the buffer plan of the record's template (what the moved-buffer check and the snapshot selection read from it, copied once); the finisher then decides from the plan, the hit's stages and guest memory which read-only buffers the draw moves and which allocations it snapshots, and copies every selected byte into one block without the hazard checks, which stay the committer's; a hit is not prepared without a plan, on a classification the committer would refuse, on a range that is not accessible, on a range over the upload limit (APS5_DRAW_UPLOAD_MAX) or over the snapshot budget, or when a copied guest range is not settled as the walk's ranges must be (a queued or pending write over it, or a write stamped since the walk began, or a write queued or pending when the copy began that retired by its end); the committer binds the prepared buffers of a hit whose template still has the plan they were prepared from: it runs the moved-buffer check's per-range tests and ticket use on the prepared moved list (with the accessibility test again once a mapping changed since the copy) and the snapshot selection's two tests on each prepared unmoved range, then copies the block into one upload slice, and takes today's check or selection for the draw on any refusal, also when a depth guard may hold samples of a selected range or was set up or dropped since the copy (APS5_VERIFY_DIRECT_BUFFERS checks it); the third `[direct-commits]` line of APS5_PIPELINE_STATS, printed when one of its counts is not zero, counts the hits prepared, the draws that bound prepared buffers, the hits not prepared by reason (no plan, classification, not accessible, over the upload limit, size budget, not settled), the prepared hits the committer did not bind by reason (plan differs, moved-range hazard, unmoved-range hazard, depth guards, dropped: the commit did not get as far as the prepared buffers), so that the hits prepared are the ones used, not used and verified, the block bytes per prepared hit (alignment padding included) and the verify's checked, differing, memory-changed and unwatched-memory hits, and the `[direct-draws]` line times the step as `buffers`; a draw's identity is its shape record's final shape key (the masked register state with the attachments, the device serial and the shader set) and its draw parameters (index address, count and size, indexed, instance count, first vertex, first instance and initiator flags), kept in a table of 8192 sets of 4 identities that only the worker writes; the fetch plan keeps the stage's compiled result as a template with the words its resource walk read (split into runs by the user word each run is based on), the run words that are data: flattened constants read as pure leaves, and the base word of each read-only buffer descriptor found once in the read words, with its record count when that value too is found nowhere else and every flattened copy of it lies in a whole copy of the descriptor, and their copies in the flattened SRT, while no flattened word lacks a source among the read words or user words and every bound buffer descriptor's base has one, the user words that may move (run bases, read-only buffer descriptor words, base words of inline read-only buffer descriptors that the walk also reads through, whose runs move with the base and whose bound descriptor words are patched when the base value appears nowhere else and no other read lands in the buffer, words forwarded to push constants or shader data, and vertex fetch table words) and the template's vertex stage info; the worker installs the record when the finisher ring retires that job, and an identity holds up to 8 records with different content or plan structure, each keeping, for each record it was built beside that no probe word taken so far tells apart, up to 2 probe words where its run words differ from that record's (8 at most), relative to a run's user word for runs based on one; a later draw of the identity reads its user words from the job, goes through the records newest first, passes over those whose user words cannot move to the draw's or whose probe words differ in memory (counted as content differs) and fetches the others until one answers or 2 did not: a fetch moves each based run by its user word, reads and compares the runs as today's draw-cache hit does (pending writes, labels, storage images and forget serial), takes the differing data words, patches them, the moved read-only buffer descriptors and the forwarded user words into side words beside the template, decodes its vertex stage info again and patches moved vertex buffer descriptors into the side words as an input refetch, folds its vertex and instance offsets, rechecks the memory it read after the capture check hook, and queues the commit with the record's content key, fragment output mask and write ranges; address-based stages also carry the read words as snapshots; any failed check counts a miss and runs today's path for the draw, which may build another record: none when a record with the same content and plan structure was among the records the draw went through, but a record whose twin lay past them is rebuilt and goes first; the committer records a hit through the record's committer half: the first commit of a record runs today's draw, which builds a draw recipe for the record (with per-draw data moves, also when APS5_LATE_MS is unset), and later hits record from it without a resource-cache lookup or a pipeline, shape or framebuffer search, checking the device, the template (its texture proof, removed from the cache when it fails), its moved read-only buffers (a moved range proven at the record's last commit is trusted while the recorder, the pending storage serial, the allocation registry and the collect epoch are unchanged and no pending write was noted over it since), the pipeline and framebuffer, and the resident targets, which are proven once per render pass: while the pass that the record last drew in is still open and the collect epoch, proof serial, pending storage serial, depth surface serials and each target's upload version are unchanged, the targets are taken as they are; a template without buffer writes, storage writes, copied writes, leases, address tables, heap stages or fault checks keeps its objects once per batch, notes its pending reads once per batch and notes each target once per render pass while the target's last note in the pass covered the same slices and nothing changed it since; its moved constants are bound with push descriptors when one of its descriptor sets fits the device's push descriptor limit without image tables, update-after-bind or dynamic buffers: that set is pushed on every hit through a pipeline built for the push layout (its libraries keyed apart, never journaled), the other set is bound as before; any failed committer check runs today's draw, whose new recipe replaces the committer half; a new identity takes the place of one not seen for more than 3 presents first, then of one without a record, then of the least recently seen, records are capped at APS5_DRAW_RECORD_MIB, and they are dropped with the switch copy, a device change (a new device serial), a replaced shape record, or a verify difference, but kept while the finisher ring is left between lean segments, which also refuses the identity until the switch copy moves; a record pins its shape and its template results and is freed only once the finisher front passes every job that carried it and its commits ran; `[draw-records]` lines count, every 10 s and at each A/B phase event while lean draws ran with records or the mode changed, the draws, the hits and their share, input refetches, hits on today's path (the verified ones), hits that relocated a base and value user word, hits that patched a moved read-only buffer base or record count, the misses by reason (new identity, second sighting, build pending, shape replaced, refused, content differs, generation moved, not queued, keyless, no fetch plan, user word moved, fetch gate, image views moved, vertex layout differs, moved buffer fields, memory changed, fetch error), the draws that ran without records (today's draw, indirect, lean front verify, inline) and the records built, records rebuilt past the candidates, fetch plans built and refused, builds refused, records installed, builds dropped, identities evicted, records cleared, record mode changes and demotions, with the identities, records and bytes held, then the committer's recorded hits (pushed, moved without a push, targets held by the pass, kept once per batch, target notes skipped, moved buffers trusted, committer halves built, verified on today's path) and its misses (no committer half, not recordable, targets, template gone, objects gone, texture proof, moved buffers, no recipe built, demoted), the line also printed while only the stage plans of APS5_STAGE_PLANS reach the committer half; | `APS5_EXACT_ROBUST_LOADS_ALIGN4` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | off unless set to a non-zero value; then, on a device with `robustBufferAccess2` whose `robustStorageBufferAccessSizeAlignment` is 4, a startup probe runs scalar and vector dword loads inside, at the end of and past storage buffer ranges of 1 to 4100 bytes (out-of-range modes 3 and 2, ranges ending inside a larger array and at its end) through the guest buffer route, once with today's descriptor-range comparison and once without it, and counts its workgroups; when every load gives the same word, direct buffer loads leave out the comparison and clamp their element index below 2^30; descriptor ranges stay as they are; prints a `[float-controls] exact robust loads` line; `APS5_NO_EXACT_ROBUST_LOADS` wins |
| `APS5_EXTRA_FINISHERS_UNPINNED` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Draw/DrawPipeline.cpp` |  | the fourth and fifth draw finisher threads (`APS5_FOURTH_FINISHER`, `APS5_FIFTH_FINISHER`) run without a reserved core; the first three keep theirs |
| `APS5_F16_NATIVE_MINMAX` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, on a device with native f16, a startup probe compiles `v_pk_min_f16`, `v_pk_max_f16`, `v_min_f16` and `v_max_f16` with GLSL `NMin` and `NMax` in which a NaN second operand hands back the first operand per lane, and compares them with the integer order today's f16 min and max use, over all 2^32 operand pairs in both lanes, in five float modes with IEEE_MODE clear and f16 input denormals kept, compiled with the compute target of wave32 programs; each of its dispatches counts its workgroups, and a count other than the number dispatched fails the probe. When no case differs, compute programs, the stage the probe runs in, take that form for `v_pk_min_f16`, `v_pk_max_f16`, `v_min_f16`, `v_max_f16`, `v_min3_f16` and `v_max3_f16` in those modes (`SpirvTarget::float16NativeMinMax`, set in the compute target only); an instruction with a literal or inline constant operand, and a pair with an operand computed from constants alone (an SGPR or VGPR set from a literal, for example), keep the integer order, `v_med3_f16` keeps it, and other stages keep it. Off by default because it changes the emitted compute shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. Log line `[float-controls] f16 min, max, min3 and max3 of compute programs` |
| `APS5_F16_SCALAR_ON_VECTOR` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, on a device with native f16, the native `v_add_f16`, `v_sub_f16`, `v_subrev_f16`, `v_mul_f16` and, with the device's NaN results kept, the `v_fma_f16` forms compute on the whole register as an f16 pair and merge the selected lane into the destination with one shuffle instead of extracting and inserting 16-bit fields (`SpirvTarget::float16ScalarOnVector`, every stage); a bit identity, no probe. SDWA destinations, output modifiers and wave-shared registers keep the field code. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. Log line `[float-controls] f16 scalar operations on whole registers` |
| `APS5_F16_WHOLE_REGISTER` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | set to anything but 0, on a device with native f16, the native `v_pk_add_f16`, `v_pk_mul_f16`, `v_pk_fma_f16` and `v_pk_fmac_f16` forms with the device's NaN results kept and f16 input denormals kept take each operand as one f16 pair: negate and absolute value as whole-word integer operations, op_sel as a vector shuffle, the NaN select, the fp16 overflow repair and the clamp as vector operations, with no per-lane extract or insert (`SpirvTarget::float16WholeRegister`, every stage); a bit identity, no probe. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keeps it apart. Log line `[float-controls] f16 packed operations on whole registers` |
| `APS5_F32_NATIVE_UNARY` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | unset, empty or a value that reads as zero (`0`, `00`, `0x0`) leaves it off; otherwise a startup probe compiles `v_rcp_f32`, `v_rcp_iflag_f32`, `v_rsq_f32`, `v_sqrt_f32`, `v_log_f32`, `v_exp_f32`, `v_fract_f32`, `v_sin_f32`, `v_cos_f32`, `v_floor_f32`, `v_ceil_f32`, `v_trunc_f32` and `v_rndne_f32` with today's special-case selects and without them, runs both over all 2^32 inputs in three float modes (undeclared, and 0xc0 with IEEE_MODE clear and set) with the f32 flush settings of the graphics and compute targets, in 64 dispatches of 2^26 inputs per program, mode and flush setting, checks both against today's special values on the special inputs and compares the two programs through a checksum of every result per operation; each dispatch must also report the expected invocation count, chunk base and input sum. An operation whose checks and checksums all match is emitted without its special-case selects (its NaN select stays); a program that does not compile, a dispatch with a wrong control count or a missing sentinel leaves every operation on today's form. A value that starts with 0x is the mask of operations allowed (all are probed): rcp (both forms) 0x1, rsq 0x2, sqrt 0x4, log 0x8, exp 0x10, fract 0x20, sin 0x40, cos 0x80, floor 0x100, ceil 0x200, trunc 0x400, rndne 0x800; any other value allows all. Applies only in float mode 0xc0 (or undeclared) with that MODE unchanged at run time, and not to operands that are constant at translation. Not probed: operands that become constant only in the device compiler (it may fold the special values to other NaN bits), wave64 and the graphics stages. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keys it. Log line `[float-controls] f32 unary operations without special-case selects` |
| `APS5_F32_WIDE_HARDWARE_FLUSH` | string | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | unset, empty or a value that reads as zero (`0`, `00`, `0x0`) leaves it off; otherwise, when the startup f32 denormal flush probe chose the hardware flush and the device declares `DenormFlushToZero`, a startup probe compiles `v_fract_f32` with today's emulated flush of its operand and result and with the hardware flush instead, runs both over all 2^32 inputs in three float modes (undeclared, and 0xc0 with IEEE_MODE clear and set), in 64 dispatches of 2^26 inputs per program and mode, checks each against the fract of the flushed input, flushed, and compares the two programs through a checksum of every result; each dispatch must also report the expected invocation count, chunk base and input sum. When every check, checksum, control count and sentinel passes, `v_fract_f32` leaves the denormal flush to the hardware. A value that starts with 0x is the mask of classes allowed (0x1 fract, the only class); any other value allows it. Applies only in float mode 0xc0 (or undeclared) with that MODE unchanged at run time, and not to operands that are constant at translation. It relies on the device flushing denormal operands, which Vulkan leaves optional, as the hardware flush of `v_floor_f32` and `v_ceil_f32` already does; not probed: operands that become constant only in the device compiler, wave64 and the graphics stages. Off by default because it changes the emitted shaders; the shader disk cache keys it. Log line `[float-controls] f32 fract with the hardware denormal flush` |
| `APS5_F16_NATIVE_WIDEN` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | off unless set to a non-zero value; then, on a device whose `denormBehaviorIndependence` is 32_BIT_ONLY and that offers DenormPreserve for 16-bit and 64-bit floats, a startup probe compares `UnpackHalf2x16` with its NaN repair under DenormPreserve 16 and 64 with the integer decode for all 65,536 halves in four float modes and counts its workgroups, before the other f16 probes; when none differs, f16 to f32 conversions whose NaN bits are used take `UnpackHalf2x16` with the NaN repair, conversions read only by comparisons take `UnpackHalf2x16` alone, conversions that feed f16 arithmetic computed in f32 keep the integer decode, and shaders that declare DenormPreserve 16 also declare it for 64-bit floats; prints a `[float-controls] f16 to f32 widening` line |
| `APS5_F16_PACK_TOWARD_ZERO` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | off unless set to a non-zero value; then, on a device with native f16 conversions, a startup probe compares GLSL `PackHalf2x16` with the emulated toward-zero narrowing of `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` for all 2^32 f32 inputs in both halves, in five float modes, and counts its workgroups; when none differs, `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` is emitted as `PackHalf2x16` except in float modes that flush f16 results and where an operand is a constant or could be folded to one by the device compiler; prints a `[float-controls] f32 to f16 toward-zero narrowing` line |
| `APS5_FAULT_SITES` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/GuestMemory.cpp` |  | on Windows, counts the guest write faults on armed pages by faulting instruction (up to 4096 sites; faults that find no slot count as dropped) and, with APS5_STAGE_STATS, prints after the `[arming]` line a `[fault-sites]` line with the main module's base and the site, fault and dropped counts, then the 256 most frequent sites as `[fault-site]` lines with their count, module and offset, all since start-up |
| `APS5_FIFTH_FINISHER` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | a fifth draw finisher thread takes queued finisher jobs too, under the same in-order commit rule as `APS5_SECOND_FINISHER`; it reserves a core like the first three unless `APS5_EXTRA_FINISHERS_UNPINNED` is set, and a thread whose switch is unset takes no jobs (it keeps running until the finisher stops); APS5_AB can toggle it |
| `APS5_FILL_CHAIN` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | seeds and doubles the fill pattern in place in the import |
| `APS5_FILL_CLEAR_EXACT_ONLY` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Memory/BufferFill.cpp` |  | turns a fill into a clear only when it covers the target exactly and nothing else |
| `APS5_FILL_SYNC` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | waits for every recorded store over the fill's range |
| `APS5_FINISHER_DEPOSIT` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | in the lean finisher ring, a finisher job that queues a commit item while an earlier job has not finished deposits the item in its own ring slot instead of waiting for its turn, and goes on with the rest of the job and the next job; whichever finisher thread finds the front job finished with deposits queues them to the draw committer in the order the job made them, and only then does the ring's front move past the job, so the committer sees the items in job order, a drain of the finisher returns with every item queued, and a deposited item's writes stay held by the ring until it is queued; a job at its turn queues directly, after its own deposits, and so does a job that waits for its turn for another reason (a committer drain, a same-key or read-order wait, the GPU lock for an inline draw); a job's own lookups of the pipeline (busy, overlaps, stored bytes, labels, read ranges, write watches) count its deposited items as queued; a forwarding thread that finds the committer queue full waits for room like a job at its turn; a failure of a forwarded item is reported like a failed job; the job queue mode of the finisher keeps the turn wait; the `[draw]` line of APS5_PIPELINE_STATS counts the items deposited and the items finisher jobs queued at their turn, and APS5_THREAD_REPORT's `[threads]` line counts the finisher turn waits and the items forwarded from deposits with their time and committer room waits; APS5_AB can toggle it |
| `APS5_FLIP_INFLIGHT` | integer | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` | 1 | number of presentations that may trail on the GPU (0, 1 or 2) |

| `APS5_FLUSH_WAIT_WHOLE_RANGE` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp` |  | waits on FlushPending for an unfinished writer anywhere in the flushed range instead of only over the pending layers it stores and the shadowed units it publishes |
| `APS5_FORCE_SDR` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/HostHdr.cpp` | unset | suppresses advertised HDR availability while retaining detected host capabilities; uses the existing SDR presentation path |
| `APS5_FORCE_STRUCTURIZER_FALLBACK` | presence | `core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/Structurizer.cpp` |  | makes the structurizer use the reach-flag fallback for every graph that supports it instead of trying cloning first; the control-flow tests then expect that fallback |
| `APS5_FOURTH_FINISHER` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | a fourth draw finisher thread takes queued finisher jobs too, under the same in-order commit rule as `APS5_SECOND_FINISHER`; it reserves a core like the first three unless `APS5_EXTRA_FINISHERS_UNPINNED` is set, and a thread whose switch is unset takes no jobs (it keeps running until the finisher stops); APS5_AB can toggle it |

| `APS5_HEAP_CENSUS_FRAME` | integer | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/HostAllocator.cpp` | 0 | with APS5_HEAP_CENSUS, a block of 1 MiB or more is counted under the frame this many levels above the caller of `operator new` (1 is the function that called the allocating one, such as the owner of a vector that grows), and each site line also prints the two frames above that one; 0 keys every block by the caller of `operator new` |
| `APS5_DRIVER_HOST_CENSUS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/DriverHostCensus.cpp` |  | census: the Vulkan instance and device are created with allocation callbacks, so the card driver's host allocations go through the AGC driver and are counted by allocation scope and, sampled once per 256 KiB, by the first AGC driver frame below the card driver's frames, found by unwinding the allocating thread's stack; prints `[driver-host]` lines every 30 s; it replaces the allocator the card driver uses, so it is never set in a speed measurement |

| `APS5_IMPORT_AHEAD` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | host imports of registered guest memory are made ahead of their first use on background threads (APS5_IMPORT_AHEAD_THREADS), started by the first address-based build, GPU mapping or finisher lookup of existing imports with the switch set and stopped for good at shutdown. The threads sleep until a request, a registry change (reported through the allocations invalidator; rescanned at most once a millisecond) or the keep time of a finished import wakes them, and plan, for each readable GPU range that changed, the import an address-based build makes of it (the same run of ranges, budget and refusal rules), and for a finisher's in-place read that finds no import the one a descriptor upload makes; a finisher miss takes no lock and wakes no thread when its 8 MiB block was asked for since the import set last changed, when 64 such requests already wait, or when such a plan was refused by the budget or APS5_IMPORT_AHEAD_MIB and neither the import set nor the imports made ahead changed since; a plan that would replace an import a build holds, pass the budget counting the imports made ahead, pass APS5_IMPORT_AHEAD_MIB, cover pages that are not committed writable private memory, leave the write watch, or lie under a live or pending storage image is left to its requester. An import thread plans under the imports mutex with a registry lease taken after it, takes the map-time import gate's stripes there without waiting (a busy gate is retried 8 times over about 6 ms with nothing held, then left to the requester), and holds the stripes only across the import's driver calls and write-tracking walks; it pins the run's ranges only while its own import is in flight or finished. A planned import makes the same driver calls as an import made by its requester (the host pointer's vkAllocateMemory and, for a settled import, its settle read) inside an import window of the write tracking that releases the tracker mutex across the driver calls: tracker calls over the window's 64 KiB blocks wait for it, others go on; it holds neither the imports mutex nor the GPU mutex. A finished import joins the import set at once when it replaces no import and the registry still yields the same run; otherwise it waits, at most APS5_IMPORT_AHEAD_KEEP_MS, for the import call that makes exactly that run, which takes it over instead of calling the driver. An import call over an import in flight waits for it, a guest unmap or protection change of its ranges waits for it and releases it, and device teardown drains them; imports in flight or finished outside an import call's run count against that call's host import budget and the heap budget report counts them as imports, so imports made ahead and the import set together stay within the budget; an import call that makes its run itself releases a finished import of the same run; the GPU mapping observer no longer imports at map time while the threads serve the device. An import its requester still makes itself (none made ahead, made ahead for another run, an alias, an import that leaves the watch) makes its driver calls in the same kind of window when it stays watched. Prints an `[import-ahead]` line for the first 16 events and every 64th, and every 10 s with `APS5_PROFILE_DRAW`: imports made ahead, put in the import set, taken over, released unused and failed, their driver time, the imports their requester still made itself (those holding the GPU lock, and why), the waits for an import in flight, the tracker calls that waited for a window, the MiB pinned ahead now and at most, the storage image publications that waited for an import's gate stripes (imports made ahead and at map time), and the plans not made by reason. Imports made ahead are not counted in the `[gpu] host imports at shutdown` requester totals. Exact: the same imports of the same runs, so the same bytes; only on which thread and when the driver calls happen changes. Runtime switch: turned off, the threads plan nothing and release the finished imports not yet taken over at their next wake, at the latest at the keep time; imports in flight finish and are still waited for. |

| `APS5_LEAN_FRONT` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | the queue-0 worker runs the lean front end on the pipelined draw path: it reads the switch once at the start of each segment (a submission, or a REWIND tail before it waits for the tail's release) and keeps the segment, and the submission it runs, in that mode, so a change takes effect at the next segment; a lean segment reads APS5_XP_KEEP_DRAWS, APS5_BESIDE_SAMPLE_DUMPS and APS5_XP_STREAM_SEGMENTS from a copy of the runtime switches taken when the switch generation last moved; the frame header's walk over its released REWIND tails keeps the copy it makes of each tail, and a lean tail runs that copy (with the next tail's REWIND control dword read again) unless the switch generation moved, a driver store (a store the driver itself made, never a CPU write, which a released tail does not expect) landed on any dword the copy came from, or those pages were forgotten since; the register lists of a lean REWIND tail go into one flat array per tail, read with today's guard and flush hook per list but without the sampled read-caller count unless APS5_PROFILE_DRAW or APS5_TRACE_UNREADABLE is set; a list of at most 256 pairs in write-watched memory is kept in a cache keyed by its address, mode dword and count and served again while the write tracker shows no write over its bytes since it was read (the hooks still run first, and all hits of a tail are checked under one tracker lock; a 64 KiB block where two lists changed or could not be collected skips the cache for 4096 tails); a tail released after the header's walk bumps the collect epoch before its lists are read rather than when it starts; the cache is off with APS5_LEAN_NO_LIST_CACHE, APS5_SUBMISSION_EPOCH_EACH, APS5_NO_COLLECT_MEMO or without write watching; buffers of finished lean tails are reused, keeping at most 64 MiB of spare buffers and none over 4 MiB; the copies kept at the header take their buffers from that pool, and each walk of the header gives the buffers of the copy slots it leaves unused back to it, so beyond the pool only the copies of the last walk hold buffers; a chain that starts lean runs its REWIND tails one after another in a loop rather than nesting each tail's execution inside the previous one: each prepared tail lives in a reused segment slot until the draw finisher has retired every job its segment handed over, the finisher is settled once at the end of the chain as today, and a tail latched in today's mode runs nested the way today's path runs it until a later tail starts a lean loop again; each lean segment's packets are classified once into a packet table, a lean REWIND tail's in the same walk that validates it, and the lean packet loop runs from that table: it calls the label, store and dump checks and the packet memory step only for the packets they act on, reuses a register list packet's committer overlap verdict from the finisher check when no finisher drain ran in between, sets the current packet for guest-memory attribution only when it changes, and a lean tail's flip room waits and flip, rendering wait and held wait reservations visit only the table's positions instead of the whole tail; the label flush between packets runs only while deferred labels are queued, at the batch cap, once a pending label's deadline (APS5_LABEL_FLUSH_US) or the 250 us completion reap interval has passed by the performance counter (a deadline converts to a counter value at or before it), and on every packet with APS5_NO_LABEL_BATCH_SUBMIT, APS5_NO_LABEL_BATCHING, APS5_LABEL_OWN_LOCK, APS5_REAP_EACH_PACKET or APS5_LABEL_TRY_EACH_PACKET; outside those switches and queued labels, a flush the gate lets through decides from the pending label, completions, recorded work and last reap the gate read; the packet and REWIND timers run only while a performance trace or the timing log records them, and worker state scopes, draw packet times, packet profiles and capture-trace lines only while APS5_THREAD_REPORT, APS5_PROFILE_DRAW, APS5_CAPTURE_TRACE, APS5_TRACE_GPU or APS5_TRACE_DRAWS asks for them; APS5_THREAD_REPORT's queue-0 worker submissions then count each lean segment once, from its start to the next segment's start; a lean worker waiting for a REWIND tail's release sleeps until sceAgcRewindPatchSetRewindState releases it with a kick or 200 us pass, and on Windows a lean worker that polls for completions between submissions wakes when a submission arrives; a lean segment hands its direct draws to the draw finishers as plain jobs in a ring the finishers take from without a lock (taken in order through a shared counter, retired in order, and parked only when idle), and the segment's other finisher jobs go through the same ring; the finishers switch between that ring and today's job queue only while they are idle; each draw's masked register state (the shape the draw cache already keys data hits by) finds a shape record, kept per device, that holds a decode of the shape built once on the worker, the shader set and device parts of the draw key, the precheck verdict for each index size (kept only when the precheck reads nothing outside the draw key registers, and a shape that needs a metadata pass, a depth copy, a depth render control or a rejection keeps today's draw), the render targets the draw writes and its PA_CL_VS_OUT_CNTL; a shape qualifies only when every program is a registered program with a header whose code writes no memory and the decoders read nothing outside the draw key registers, so the draw's held writes are exactly its targets; the worker copies the draw's 132 user words and six resource registers into the job, finishes the exact draw key from them and hands the job over, and the finisher runs the draw cache lookup, relocation, data entry refresh, vertex stage decode and today's finish from the job, waiting for its turn where an earlier unfinished finisher job may write what it reads or insert its key (APS5_SPECULATIVE_LOOKUP and APS5_XP_REPLAY act on that wait as on today's lookup drain); shape records are checked against each new shader registry and dropped with the switch copy; indirect draws, draws while labels are deferred, and draws under APS5_PROFILE_DRAW, APS5_LOCKED_DRAW_PREPARE, APS5_DUMP_DRAW_SHADERS, APS5_DUMP_DRAW_SLOT1, APS5_TRACE_DRAW_PROGRAMS, APS5_TRACE_SHADER_CORRELATION, APS5_TRACE_DRAW_CACHE, APS5_VERIFY_DRAW_DATA_HITS, APS5_VERIFY_DRAW_RECIPE, APS5_VERIFY_DRAW_ENTRIES, APS5_VERIFY_REGISTER_KEYS, APS5_RELOCATION_DRAIN, APS5_NO_DRAW_KEY or APS5_NO_DRAW_SRT_ENTRIES keep today's draw; other queues, the unpipelined path (APS5_PIPELINED_DRAWS=0) and suspend points keep today's path; `[lean-front]` lines count, every 10 s and at each A/B phase event while lean segments ran or the front changed mode, the lean and today's segments, mode changes, switch copies and the deepest nesting of lean segments, followed by the lean parts' own counters that moved (segments looped, prepared copies used, missed and refused, late tail epoch hoists, lean register lists, list cache hits, misses, invalidations, admits, bypasses and evictions, flush checks skipped and called, REWIND waits, kick wakes, kick timeouts, tails released without a kick, kicks with no waiter, idle polls woken by a submission, packet tables built, segment slots created and held past their segment, settles that waited, lean draws, draws and indirect draws on today's path, shape records built, restamped and evicted, shapes refused, finisher ring room waits, finisher waits for earlier jobs, finisher same-key waits, finisher store switches, lean draws that ran the precheck); APS5_AB can toggle it |

| `APS5_NO_ADDRESS_DRAW_REUSE` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp` |  | builds every read-only address-based draw anew instead of reusing a build whose bindings all lie in its cached address space |
| `APS5_NO_ADDRESS_READ_PASSES` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp` |  | stops a draw that only reads through its buffer descriptors from counting as read-only, so it breaks the pass instead of continuing the producer's |
| `APS5_NO_ADDRESS_SPACE_CACHE` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp` |  | disables the address-space cache so every build rebuilds its space |
| `APS5_NO_ADJACENT_GENERATION` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp` |  | leaves adjacent pending images at their generation during a write-back, as before |

| `APS5_NO_ARM_RESIDENT` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/RuntimeSwitches.cpp` |  | on Windows, turns resident arming off, so collects use GetWriteWatch only: a clearing collect no longer write-protects a settled, write-watched span of a resident run of at least 256 KiB that no guest store reached since it was last armed and remembers it clean for later collects to skip; switching it on mid-run resumes every armed run at the next write walk without marking it written; APS5_AB can toggle it |

| `APS5_NO_BARRIER_ELISION` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Recorder.cpp` |  | records every leading barrier instead of merging it into the following submission |
| `APS5_NO_BDA_STAGING` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp` |  | binds the written elements of address-based compute builds in place in the host imports instead of staging them device-local |
| `APS5_NO_BDA_TABLE_CACHE` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/BdaResources.cpp` |  | builds every BDA descriptor table instead of reusing the recent-table cache |

| `APS5_NO_CARRIED_SHADOWS` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp` |  | copies a staged region in from its host import on every use instead of from the previous use's device-local shadow when no write reached the range since that use's copy-back |
| `APS5_NO_CHANGED_COPY_BACK` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp` |  | copies a GPU-copied buffer's whole range back into its import instead of only the bytes the shader changed |
| `APS5_NO_CLEAR_HAND_OVER` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp` |  | writes other images' pending results back at a clear-key storage refresh instead of handing them over to the refreshed image |
| `APS5_NO_CLEARED_VIEW` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp` |  | keeps fast-cleared surfaces as CPU snapshots instead of viewing the cleared storage image |
| `APS5_NO_COLLECT_MEMO` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/GuestMemory.cpp` |  | disables answering a collect from the per-block record of earlier walks |

| `APS5_NO_CONTEXT_CACHE` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp` |  | rebuilds the Graphics::Context on every call instead of returning the cached one |

| `APS5_NO_CPU_COPY` | presence | `core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Memory/BufferCopy.cpp` |  | disables the CPU copy path of the copy HLE |
